Appendix A
Additional Related Work

Our work is related to a growing line of work calibration in decision-making settings. The seminal work of
Foster and Vohra [1999]
showed that a decision maker who best responds to calibrated forecasts obtain diminishing internal regret. More recent studies extend this result by proposing refined notions of calibration that are more efficient to achieve and offer fine-grained regret guarantees
[
Noarov et al., 2023
,
Kleinberg et al., 2023
,
Hu and Wu, 2024
,
Roth and Shi, 2024
,
Fishelson et al., 2025
,
Luo et al., 2025
,
Tang et al., 2025
]
. Building on (multi)calibration,
Gopalan et al. [2021]
introduced the notion of omniprediction, which aims to construct a single predictor that guarantees no worse loss than a family of predetermined benchmarks for all the downstream receivers in a class, followed by
Gopalan et al. [2022]
,
Gopalan et al. [2024]
,
Garg et al. [2024]
,
Dwork et al. [2024]
,
Okoroafor et al. [2025]
,
Lu et al. [2025]
. In contrast to these works, we not only aim to achieve a specific notion of calibration (decision calibration, in our case), but also seek to approximately maximize the sender’s utility among all such calibrated predictors.

Our work shares the common goal of replacing prior knowledge with data, aligning with many works in mechanism design, such as auction design
[
Balcan et al., 2008
,
Cole and Roughgarden, 2014
,
Morgenstern and Roughgarden, 2015
,
Daskalakis and Syrgkanis, 2016
,
Syrgkanis, 2017
,
Dudík et al., 2020
,
Fu and Lin, 2020
]
, Stackelberg game
[
Balcan et al., 2015
,
Camara et al., 2020
,
Collina et al., 2024
]
, algorithm discrimination
[
Cummings et al., 2020
]
and recommendation system
[
Immorlica et al., 2018
]
.

More broadly, a growing body of work in economics aims to relax the assumption of perfect prior knowledge rather than replace it entirely, such as relaxing the prior to some kind of approximate agreement on the distribution
[
Artemov et al., 2013
,
Ollár and Penta, 2017
]
and robustness to prior distribution
[
Dworczak and Pavan, 2022
,
Kosterina, 2022
]
. In contrast to these works, we adopt a data-driven approach to address the challenge of an unknown prior distribution.

Appendix B
Global Optimality for Finite-size
\mathcal{X}

In this section, we consider the case that
{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{X}\right|}{|\mathcal{X}|}{|\mathcal{X}|}{|\mathcal{X}|}}<\infty
,
\mathcal{Y}=\{0,1\}
and there is one receiver, i.e.
N=1
. We show that it is sufficient to consider predictions in an instance-dependent discretization set. Fix any receiver’s utility
v
, we slightly abuse notation, let
\mathcal{A}=\{a_{1},\cdots,a_{m}\}
denote the receiver’s action set. Let
J_{i}=\{p\in[0,1]:a_{i}=\argmax\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Ber(p)}[v(a,y)]\}
for any
i\in[m]
. If there are ties, the receiver breaks ties in favor of the sender. Then we know that
J_{i}
is an interval on [0,1] for any
i\in[m]
and
\{J_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}
is a partition of
[0,1]
. Let
\mathcal{Z}
be the set of thresholds of adjacent best response intervals. We know
{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{Z}\right|}{|\mathcal{Z}|}{|\mathcal{Z}|}{|\mathcal{Z}|}}\leq m-1
. Let
\Theta=\{\Pr[y=1\mid x]:\forall x\in\mathcal{X}\}
. We know
{\mathchoice{\left|\Theta\right|}{|\Theta|}{|\Theta|}{|\Theta|}}\leq{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{X}\right|}{|\mathcal{X}|}{|\mathcal{X}|}{|\mathcal{X}|}}
.

Definition B.1

(Instance-dependent discretization)
.

For any positive
\epsilon<\min_{i\in m}\mathrm{len}(J_{i})
, define discretized set
S_{\epsilon}
of space [0,1] as

S_{\epsilon}\triangleq(\{0,\epsilon,2\epsilon,\cdots\}\cap[0,1])\cup\mathcal{Z}\cup\Theta.

Definition B.2

(Discretized predictor set)
.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}=\{h:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow S_{\epsilon}\}
be the set of all possible predictors whose predictions are always in the instance-dependent discretization set
S_{\epsilon}
.

Note that
\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}
is finite with size
{\mathchoice{\left|S_{\epsilon}\right|}{|S_{\epsilon}|}{|S_{\epsilon}|}{|S_{\epsilon}|}}^{\mathcal{X}}
. The following theorem shows that any randomized predictor in
\Delta(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ALL}})
can be converted to a randomized predictor in
\Delta(\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon})
without changing the sender’s utility and increase the decision calibration error up to
\epsilon
.

Theorem B.1
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, for any randomized predictor
f\in\Delta(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ALL}})
that is
\gamma
-decision calibrated, we can construct a randomized predictor
f^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon})
such that (1) the sender obtains the same expected utility under
f
and
f^{\prime}
(2)
f^{\prime}
is
(\gamma+\epsilon)
-decision calibrated.

Proof.

Define the range of
f
as
\mathrm{range}(f)=\bigcup_{h\in\supp{f}}\mathrm{range}(h)
. Consider the following rounding function
r:\mathrm{range}(f)\rightarrow S_{\epsilon}
:

r(v)=p\quad v\in J_{i},~p=\arg\inf_{p^{\prime}\in(S_{\epsilon}\cap J_{i})}{\mathchoice{\left|p^{\prime}-v\right|}{|p^{\prime}-v|}{|p^{\prime}-v|}{|p^{\prime}-v|}}.

For any
h\sim f\in\Delta(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ALL}})
, we construct
h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}
as
h^{\prime}:x\mapsto r(h(x))
. Let
f^{\prime}
be the distribution over such
h^{\prime}
. By the definition of
S_{\epsilon}
, we have that (1)
{\mathchoice{\left|v-r(v)\right|}{|v-r(v)|}{|v-r(v)|}{|v-r(v)|}}<\epsilon
(2) the receiver has the same best response under
v
and
r(v)
. Therefore, the sender obtains
the same expected utility under
f
and
f^{\prime}
. And we have

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\left|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h^{\prime}(x),a)]\right|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h^{\prime}(x),a)]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h^{\prime}(x),a)]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h^{\prime}(x),a)]|}}

\displaystyle={\mathchoice{\left|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]\right|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathchoice{\left|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]\right|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathchoice{\left|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(h^{\prime}(x)-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]\right|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(h^{\prime}(x)-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(h^{\prime}(x)-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(h^{\prime}(x)-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}}

\displaystyle={\mathchoice{\left|\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]\right|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathchoice{\left|\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(h^{\prime}(x)-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]\right|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(h^{\prime}(x)-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(h^{\prime}(x)-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(h^{\prime}(x)-h(x))\cdot b(h(x),a)]|}}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma+\epsilon.

∎

This theorem implies that, at least in the special case considered in this section, to find the optimal decision-calibrated predictor randomized over all deterministic predictors, it suffices to consider a finite subset of deterministic predictors
\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}
.

Appendix C
No Regret Guarantees of Decision Calibration

C.1
No Regret Guarantees of Strict Decision Calibration

We first prove that approximate decision calibrated predictor gives the downstream agent no swap regret best responding to it.
Noarov et al. [2023]
proved it for deterministic predictor in the online calibration setting. We provide our proof for randomized predictor in the batch settting here for completeness.
See
2.1

Proof.

We prove the result for any receiver
i\in[N]
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}

When
f
is
\epsilon
-decision-calibrated, we know that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a}\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y-h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y-h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y-h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y-h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{a}L\epsilon=L|A|\epsilon.

Similarly we can prove that

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}\leq L|A|\epsilon.

Since
b_{i}(h(x),a)
plays the best response given the prediction
h(x)
, we know that

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}\leq 0.

Putting them together, we have

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}\leq 2L|A|\epsilon.

∎

Definition C.1

(Type Regret)
.

We say that a predictor
f
achieves
\epsilon
-type regret if, for any receiver
i,i^{\prime}\in[N]
,
\phi:A\rightarrow A
,

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}\leq\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}+\epsilon.

Now we introduce a different notion of regret, named
type regret
. Type regret is first introduced by
Zhao et al. [2021]
. Intuitively, it says that once the predictor gets decision calibrated with respect to a class of utility functions of receivers, the receivers will have no regret best responding according to another receiver’s utility function instead their own.
Zhao et al. [2021]
proved that decision calibrated predictor achieves no type regret for the receivers. Here we state and prove the result for randomized predictors.

Theorem C.1

(No Type Regret via Decision Calibration)
.

If a predictor
f
is
\epsilon
-decision calibrated, then it satisfies
2L|A|\epsilon
-type regret.

Proof.

We prove the result for any receiver
i,i^{\prime}\in[N]
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}

When
f
is
\epsilon
-decision-calibrated, we know that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a}\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y-h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y-h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y-h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y-h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{a}L\epsilon=L|A|\epsilon.

Similarly we can prove that

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}\leq L|A|\epsilon.

Since
b_{i}(h(x),a)
plays the best response given the prediction
h(x)
, we know that

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}\leq 0.

Putting them together, we have

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}\leq 2L|A|\epsilon.

∎

We also introduce a new notion of regret which we call swap-type-regret, which intuitively capture the case where the receivers can first pretend that they were another receiver and then swap the corresponding best-response action. We formally define it as follows:

Definition C.2

(Swap-Type Regret)
.

We say that a predictor
f
achieves
\epsilon
-swap-type regret if, for any receiver
i,i^{\prime}\in[N]
, mapping function
\phi:A\rightarrow A
,

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}\leq\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}+\epsilon.

Theorem C.2

(No Swap-Type Regret via Decision Calibration)
.

If a predictor
f
is
\epsilon
-decision calibrated, then it satisfies
2L|A|\epsilon
-swap-type regret.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proofs of
Theorem

2.1
and
Theorem

C.1
.
We can similarly prove that

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}\leq L|A|\epsilon,

and

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}\leq L|A|\epsilon.

From the fact that
b_{i}(h(x),a)
selects the best response action, we also have

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}\leq 0.

Putting them together completes the proof.
∎

C.2
No Regret Guarantees of Smoothed Decision Calibration

We provide analogous result for the behavior model where the receivers follow quantal response. We first define the three notions of regret for quantal response.

Definition C.3

(Swap Regret under Quantal Response)
.

We say that a predictor
f
achieves
\epsilon
-swap regret for receivers that follow quantal response rule if, for any receiver
i\in[N]
, mapping function
\phi:A\rightarrow A
,

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}}\leq\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}}+\epsilon.

Definition C.4

(Type Regret under Quantal Response)
.

We say that a predictor
f
achieves
\epsilon
-type regret for receivers that follow quantal response rule if, for any receiver
i,i^{\prime}\in[N]
,
\phi:A\rightarrow A
,

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}\leq\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}}+\epsilon.

Definition C.5

(Swap-Type under Quantal Response)
.

We say that a predictor
f
achieves
\epsilon
-swap-type regret for receivers that follow quantal response rule if, for any receiver
i,i^{\prime}\in[N]
, mapping function
\phi:A\rightarrow A
,

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}\leq\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}}+\epsilon.

We now present the analogous no regret guarantee for quantal response receivers. Swap regret for quantal response receivers are discussed in
Roth and Shi [2024]
in the online setting for deterministic predictors. Here, we provide the result for randomized predictors in the batch setting. Type regret for quantal response receivers are discussed in
Tang et al. [2025]
for determinisitc predictors. Here, we state and prove the result for randomized predictors.

Theorem C.3
.

If a predictor
f
is
\epsilon
-smoothed-decision calibrated, then it satisfies
2L|A|\epsilon+\frac{\ln|A|+1}{\eta}
-swap/type/swap-type regret.

To prove
Theorem

C.3
, we first present a lemma proved by
Roth and Shi [2024]
, which shows that the utility that a receiver gets when they play quantal response with respect to the true outcome will be close that when they strictly best responds.

Lemma C.1

(
Roth and Shi [2024]
)
.

For any utility function
v
and
y\in\mathcal{Y}
, let
a^{*}=\argmax_{a}v(a,y)
, we have

\sum_{a}v(a,y)\tilde{b}(y,a)\geq v(a^{*},y)-\frac{\ln|A|+1}{\eta}.

Proof of
Theorem

C.3
.

We only provide proof for swap-type regret as it is the strongest. The guarantees for swap regret and type regret are simple corollaries by considering
\phi
to be the identical mapping and
i=i^{\prime}
.

For any
\phi:\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{A},i,i^{\prime}\in[N]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}}

From the definition of decision calibration, we know that

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}\leq L|A|\epsilon

and

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}}\leq L|A|\epsilon.

By
Lemma

C.1
, we know that

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}}\geq\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}-\frac{\ln|A|+1}{\eta}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(,h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(,h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(,h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i^{\prime}}(,h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(,h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(,h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(,h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(,h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(,h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(,h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(,h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(,h(x),a)]}}+\frac{\ln|A|+1}{\eta}.

Since given
h(x)
,
b_{i}(h(x),a)
is the optimal decision rule for
v_{i}
, we know that

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),h(x))\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,h(x))\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}\leq 0

Putting them together, we have that

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(\phi(a),y)\cdot b_{i^{\prime}}(h(x),a)]}}-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)\right]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}{[\sum_{a}v_{i}(a,y)\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a)]}}\leq 2L|A|\epsilon+\frac{\ln|A|+1}{\eta}.

∎

Appendix D
Missing Proofs in Section
3

See
3.2

Proof.

We use
f^{*}
to denote the optimal feasible solution to
Eq.

2
, since the constraints are satisfied, we have that
\mathcal{L}(f^{*},\hat{\lambda})\leq\mathrm{OPT}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{D},\gamma)
. We prove the theorem by considering two cases.

First, if
\hat{f}
is a feasible solution to the problem
Eq.

2
, i.e.
\mathrm{DecCE}(\hat{f})\leq\gamma\leq\gamma+\frac{1+2\epsilon}{C}
. Since
(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})
is a
\epsilon
-approximate equilibrium, we have that

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]

\displaystyle=\max_{\lambda}\mathcal{L}(\hat{f},\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})+\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq\min_{f\in\Delta(\mathcal{H})}\mathcal{L}(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})+2\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}(f^{*},\bar{\lambda})+2\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{D}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]+2\epsilon

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{OPT}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{D},\gamma).

Therefore, we have

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]\geq\mathrm{OPT}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{D},\gamma)-2\epsilon.

Second, we consider the case where
\hat{f}
is not a feasible solution to
Eq.

2
. Let
(\hat{s},\hat{i},\hat{j},\hat{a}_{i})=\argmax_{s,i,j,a_{i}}s{\mathchoice{\left(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma\right)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}}>0
, and let
\lambda^{\prime}
be the vector such that the
(\hat{s},\hat{i},\hat{j},\hat{a}_{i})
-th coordinate
\lambda^{\prime}_{\hat{s},\hat{i},\hat{j},\hat{a}_{i}}=C
, and all else coordinates are
0
, then we have that given
\lambda^{\prime}\in\argmax_{\lambda}\mathcal{L}(\hat{f},\lambda)
. Therefore, since
(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})
is a
\epsilon
-approximate equilibrium, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq\max_{\lambda}\mathcal{L}(\hat{f},\lambda)-\epsilon

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]+C\hat{s}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{\hat{j}}-y_{\hat{j}})\cdot b_{\hat{i}}(h(x),a_{\hat{i}})]-\gamma\right)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{\hat{j}}-y_{\hat{j}})\cdot b_{\hat{i}}(h(x),a_{\hat{i}})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{\hat{j}}-y_{\hat{j}})\cdot b_{\hat{i}}(h(x),a_{\hat{i}})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{\hat{j}}-y_{\hat{j}})\cdot b_{\hat{i}}(h(x),a_{\hat{i}})]-\gamma)}}-\epsilon

Therefore,

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]+C\hat{s}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{\hat{j}}-y_{\hat{j}})\cdot b_{\hat{i}}(h(x),a_{\hat{i}})]-\gamma\right)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{\hat{j}}-y_{\hat{j}})\cdot b_{\hat{i}}(h(x),a_{\hat{i}})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{\hat{j}}-y_{\hat{j}})\cdot b_{\hat{i}}(h(x),a_{\hat{i}})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{\hat{j}}-y_{\hat{j}})\cdot b_{\hat{i}}(h(x),a_{\hat{i}})]-\gamma)}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})+\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}(f^{*},\bar{\lambda})+2\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim\hat{f}}\mathbb{E}_{D}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]+2\epsilon

Since
\forall\bm{a},y,u(\bm{a},y)\in[0,1]
, we have that

C\hat{s}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{\hat{j}}-y_{\hat{j}})\cdot b_{\hat{i}}(h(x),a_{\hat{i}})]-\gamma\right)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{\hat{j}}-y_{\hat{j}})\cdot b_{\hat{i}}(h(x),a_{\hat{i}})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{\hat{j}}-y_{\hat{j}})\cdot b_{\hat{i}}(h(x),a_{\hat{i}})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{\hat{j}}-y_{\hat{j}})\cdot b_{\hat{i}}(h(x),a_{\hat{i}})]-\gamma)}}\leq 1+2\epsilon.

Thus,

\max_{s,i,j,a_{i}}s{\mathchoice{\left(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma\right)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}}\leq\frac{1+2\epsilon}{C},

and this implies that
\mathrm{DecCE}(\hat{f})\leq\gamma+\frac{1+2\epsilon}{C}.

∎

We need the following technical lemmas before proving
Lemma

3.3
. For any function
\psi:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and any dataset
D=\{(x^{(i)},y^{(i)})\}_{i=1}^{n}
, we denote the empirical expectation of
\psi
over
D
as

\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{D}[\psi(x,y)]\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(x^{(i)},y^{(i)}).

Theorem D.1
.

Fix a finite-size class of deterministic predictors
\mathcal{H}
. For any distribution
\mathcal{D}
, let
D\sim\mathcal{D}^{n}
be a dataset consisting of
n
samples
(x^{(i)},y^{(i)})
sampled i.i.d. from
\mathcal{D}
. Then for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability
1-\delta
, for every
f\in\Delta(\mathcal{H})

∎

Proof.

{\mathchoice{\left(y_{j}-h(x)_{j}\right)}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}}\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})\in[-2,2]
. By Hoeffding’s inequality, we have for any

, for any

with probability

, we have

{\mathchoice{\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left(y_{j}-h(x)_{j}\right)}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}}\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]}{[{\mathchoice{\left(y_{j}-h(x)_{j}\right)}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}}\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left(y_{j}-h(x)_{j}\right)}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}}\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]}{[{\mathchoice{\left(y_{j}-h(x)_{j}\right)}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}}\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]}{[{\mathchoice{\left(y_{j}-h(x)_{j}\right)}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}}\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]}}|}{|\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left(y_{j}-h(x)_{j}\right)}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}}\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]}{[{\mathchoice{\left(y_{j}-h(x)_{j}\right)}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}}\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]}{[{\mathchoice{\left(y_{j}-h(x)_{j}\right)}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}{(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})}}\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]}}|}}]}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}{\mathchoice{\left[\sqrt{\frac{8\ln\frac{2{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{H}\right|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}}Ndm}{\delta}}{n}}\right]}{[\sqrt{\frac{8\ln\frac{2{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{H}\right|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}}Ndm}{\delta}}{n}}]}{[\sqrt{\frac{8\ln\frac{2{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{H}\right|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}}Ndm}{\delta}}{n}}]}{[\sqrt{\frac{8\ln\frac{2{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{H}\right|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}}Ndm}{\delta}}{n}}]}}=\sqrt{\frac{8\ln\frac{2{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{H}\right|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}}Ndm}{\delta}}{n}}.

∎

Now we are ready to prove
Lemma

3.3
.
See
3.3

Proof.

This is straightforward from
Theorem

D.1
and
Theorem

D.2
, we split the budget to
\delta/2
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{D}(f,\lambda)-\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]+-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\{+,-\}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sum_{a_{i}\in A_{i}}\lambda_{s,i,j,a_{i}}s{\mathchoice{\left(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma\right)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{s\in\{+,-\}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sum_{a_{i}\in A_{i}}\lambda_{s,i,j,a_{i}}s{\mathchoice{\left(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma\right)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}}

Therefore,

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{L}_{D}(f,\lambda)-\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)\right|}{|\mathcal{L}_{D}(f,\lambda)-\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)|}{|\mathcal{L}_{D}(f,\lambda)-\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)|}{|\mathcal{L}_{D}(f,\lambda)-\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)|}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\left|\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]\right|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]|}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\{+,-\}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sum_{a_{i}\in A_{i}}\lambda_{s,i,j,a_{i}}s{\mathchoice{\left|\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]\right|}{|\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]|}{|\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot b_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]|}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\ln\frac{{\mathchoice{\left|4\mathcal{H}\right|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}}}{\delta}}{2n}}+C\sqrt{\frac{8\ln\frac{4{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{H}\right|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}}Ndm}{\delta}}{n}}.

∎

Finally we are ready to prove
Theorem

3.1
.
See
3.1

Proof.

The regret bound of Hedge algorithm is
O(C\sqrt{T\log Nmd})
, and the best response of
f
give non-positive regret. From
Lemma

3.1
and
T=O(\log(Nmd/\delta)/\epsilon^{4})
, we have that
(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})
is an
{\varepsilon}/4
-approximate equilibrium under
\mathcal{L}_{D}(f,\lambda)
. From
Lemma

3.3
, we know that with probability
1-\delta
,
\forall f\in\Delta(\mathcal{H}),\lambda\in\Lambda
,

{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)-\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)\right|}{|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)-\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)|}{|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)-\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)|}{|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)-\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)|}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\ln\frac{{\mathchoice{\left|4\mathcal{H}\right|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}}}{\delta}}{2n}}+C\sqrt{\frac{8\ln\frac{4{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{H}\right|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}}Ndm}{\delta}}{n}}.

Therefore, let
f^{\prime}=\arg\min_{f\in\Delta(\mathcal{H})}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\bar{\lambda})

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})-\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f^{\prime},\bar{\lambda})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})-\mathcal{L}_{D}(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})+\mathcal{L}_{D}(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})-\mathcal{L}_{D}(f^{\prime},\bar{\lambda})+\mathcal{L}_{D}(f^{\prime},\bar{\lambda})-\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f^{\prime},\bar{\lambda})

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon/2+2\sqrt{\frac{\ln\frac{{\mathchoice{\left|4\mathcal{H}\right|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}}}{\delta}}{2n}}+2C\sqrt{\frac{8\ln\frac{4{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{H}\right|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}}Ndm}{\delta}}{n}}.

Similarly, we can prove that

\max_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(\hat{f},\lambda)-\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})\leq\epsilon/4+2\sqrt{\frac{\ln\frac{{\mathchoice{\left|4\mathcal{H}\right|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}}}{\delta}}{2n}}+2C\sqrt{\frac{8\ln\frac{4{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{H}\right|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}}Ndm}{\delta}}{n}}.

Therefore,
(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})
is an
\epsilon/4+2\sqrt{\frac{\ln\frac{{\mathchoice{\left|4\mathcal{H}\right|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}{|4\mathcal{H}|}}}{\delta}}{2n}}+2C\sqrt{\frac{8\ln\frac{4{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{H}\right|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}{|\mathcal{H}|}}Ndm}{\delta}}{n}}
-approximate equilibrium for the payoff
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda)
under the true distribution
\mathcal{D}
. When
n\geq O(\frac{\log(|\mathcal{H}|Ndm)}{\epsilon^{4}})
, we have
(\hat{f},\bar{\lambda})
is a
\epsilon/2
-approximate equilibrium under the true distribution
\mathcal{D}
. Then, by
Lemma

3.2
, and the choice of
C
, we have

\mathrm{DecCE}(\hat{f})\leq\gamma+\frac{1+\epsilon}{C}\leq\gamma+\frac{1+\epsilon}{2/\epsilon}\leq\gamma+\frac{2}{2/\epsilon}=\gamma+\epsilon,

and

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]\geq\mathrm{OPT}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{D},\gamma)-\epsilon.

Finally by
Theorem

2.1
, the receivers who play
\eta
-quantal response obtain the stated swap regret bound.
∎

Appendix E
Missing Proofs in Section
4

See
4.1

Proof.

Fix any signaling scheme
\pi:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\Delta(S)
. Any context
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}
corresponds to a state
\theta_{x^{\prime}}=\mathbb{E}[y\mid x^{\prime}]
and hence corresponds to a distribution of signals. Any signal
s\in S
corresponds to a posterior mean
\mathbb{E}[\theta_{x^{\prime}}\mid s]
. We define the mapping
g:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{Y})
such that

\Pr[g(x)=\mathbb{E}[\theta_{x^{\prime}}\mid s]]=\pi(s\mid\theta_{x}).

We have that for any
s\in S

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[y\mid g(x)=\mathbb{E}[\theta_{x^{\prime}}\mid s]]

\displaystyle=\int_{y}y\cdot\Pr[y\mid g(x)=\mathbb{E}[\theta_{x^{\prime}}\mid s]]dy

\displaystyle=\int_{y}y\cdot{\mathchoice{\left(\int_{x}\Pr[y\mid x,s]dx\right)}{(\int_{x}\Pr[y\mid x,s]dx)}{(\int_{x}\Pr[y\mid x,s]dx)}{(\int_{x}\Pr[y\mid x,s]dx)}}dy

\displaystyle=\int_{x}{\mathchoice{\left(\int_{y}\Pr[y\mid x,s]dy\right)}{(\int_{y}\Pr[y\mid x,s]dy)}{(\int_{y}\Pr[y\mid x,s]dy)}{(\int_{y}\Pr[y\mid x,s]dy)}}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{x}\mathbb{E}[y\mid x,s]dx

\displaystyle=\int_{x}\theta_{x}\Pr[x\mid s]dx

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\theta_{x^{\prime}}\mid s]

Therefore, we can convert
g
to a randomized predictor
f\in\Delta(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ALL}})
that is perfectly calibrated.

Now consider any randomized predictor
f
that is calibrated, i.e. we have that for any
v\in\mathcal{Y}

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[y-h(x)\mid h(x)=v]=0.

Now consider a signaling scheme such that the signal set is
\mathcal{Y}
and given any state
\theta=\mathbb{E}[y\mid x]
, it sends signal
v\in\mathcal{Y}
with probability
\Pr_{h\sim f}[h(x)=v\mid\theta]
. We have that for any signal
v
, the posterior mean is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\theta\mid v]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\theta\mid h(x)=v]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[y\mid x]\mid h(x)=v]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[y\mid h(x)]

\displaystyle=v.

∎

See
4.2

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that for any
a
, event
\{b(h(x),a)=1\}
happens with non-zero probability, otherwise we could remove that action.
Since
f
is perfectly decision calibrated, we have

\max_{j\in[d]}\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})\cdot b(h(x),a)]=0.

Since
\Pr\{b(h(x),a)=1\}>0
, equivalently, we have

\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[h(x)-y|b(h(x),a)=1\right]}{[h(x)-y|b(h(x),a)=1]}{[h(x)-y|b(h(x),a)=1]}{[h(x)-y|b(h(x),a)=1]}}=0.

For any
a\in\mathcal{A}
, let
f^{\prime}_{a}:=\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[h(x)|b(h(x),a)=1\right]}{[h(x)|b(h(x),a)=1]}{[h(x)|b(h(x),a)=1]}{[h(x)|b(h(x),a)=1]}}

Consider a post-processing function
p(h(x))=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a}
.
Now we are ready to construct
f^{\prime}
, we let
f^{\prime}=p(f)
. Note that
f^{\prime}
only output at most
m
values, we only need to check the level sets of each
f^{\prime}_{a}
. Also, the set
\{y|b(y,a)=1\}
is convex, therefore
b(f^{\prime}_{a},a)=1
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})\cdot\bm{1}(h(x)=f^{\prime}_{a})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y_{j}-h(x)_{j})\cdot b(h(x),a)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y_{j}-p(h(x))_{j})\cdot b(h(x),a)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left(y_{j}-{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}}_{j}\right)}{(y_{j}-{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}}_{j})}{(y_{j}-{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}}_{j})}{(y_{j}-{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}}_{j})}}\cdot b(h(x),a)]}{[{\mathchoice{\left(y_{j}-{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}}_{j}\right)}{(y_{j}-{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}}_{j})}{(y_{j}-{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}}_{j})}{(y_{j}-{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}}_{j})}}\cdot b(h(x),a)]}{[{\mathchoice{\left(y_{j}-{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}}_{j}\right)}{(y_{j}-{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}}_{j})}{(y_{j}-{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}}_{j})}{(y_{j}-{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}{[\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}]}}_{j})}}\cdot b(h(x),a)]}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y_{j}-[b(h(x),a)f^{\prime}_{a}]_{j})\cdot b(h(x),a)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(y_{j}\cdot b(h(x),a)]-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{(x,y)\sim\mathcal{D}}[(h(x)_{j}\cdot b(h(x),a)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

∎

See
4.1

Proof.

By
Theorem

3.1
we know that given any tolerance
\gamma\geq 0
and
n\geq O{\mathchoice{\left(\frac{\ln(|\mathcal{H}|dm/\delta)}{\epsilon^{4}}\right)}{(\frac{\ln(|\mathcal{H}|dm/\delta)}{\epsilon^{4}})}{(\frac{\ln(|\mathcal{H}|dm/\delta)}{\epsilon^{4}})}{(\frac{\ln(|\mathcal{H}|dm/\delta)}{\epsilon^{4}})}}
, let
C=\frac{2}{\epsilon}
, when
PerDecCal
runs for
T=O(\ln(Nmd)/\epsilon^{4})
rounds, with probability at least
1-\delta
, it outputs
\hat{f}
that satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[u(\bm{a},y)\cdot b(h(x),\bm{a})]

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{OPT}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{D},\gamma)-\epsilon

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{OPT}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{D},0)-\epsilon.

Note that
\mathrm{OPT}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{D},0)
is the optimal sender utility achieved by the randomized predictors over
\mathcal{H}
that are perfectly decision calibrated, i.e.
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{DCAL}}(\mathcal{H})
. By
Definition

4.2
and
Lemma

4.2
,
\mathrm{OPT}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{D},0)
is equal to the optimal sender utility achieved by the predictors in
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{CAL}}(\mathcal{H})
.

Then by
Definition

4.2
and
Lemma

4.1
,
\mathrm{OPT}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{D},0)
is equal to the optimal sender utility achieved by the signaling schemes in
\Pi_{\mathcal{H}}
, i.e.
\mathrm{BayesOPT(\mu_{\mathcal{D}},\Pi_{\mathcal{H}})}
.

Putting all these together, we know that the sender’s expected utility under
\hat{f}
is greater than
\mathrm{BayesOPT(\mu_{\mathcal{D}},\Pi_{\mathcal{H}})}-\epsilon
.

∎

F.1
The algorithm for Infinite Hypothesis Class

Lagrangian and Minimax Game

Similarly, we can introduce the Lagrangian and restrict the Lagrangian variables to be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\min_{f\in\Delta{\mathcal{H}}}\max_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathcal{D}}(f,\lambda):=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot\tilde{b}(h(x),\bm{a})]

(5)

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\{+,-\}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sum_{a_{i}\in A_{i}}\lambda_{s,i,j,a_{i}}s{\mathchoice{\left(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma\right)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{f}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h(x)_{j}-y_{j})\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}(h(x),a_{i})]-\gamma)}}.

Now we present our oracle efficient algorithm
SmPerDecCal
.

Algorithm 2

SmPerDecCal

0:

A set of samples
D
, ERM oracle
\mathrm{ERM}(D,\lambda)
, dual bound
C
and tolerance
\gamma
.

1:

Initialize
\lambda_{1}=\frac{C}{2Nmd}\bm{1}
.

2:

for

t=1,\cdots,T

do

3:

Learner best responds to
\lambda_{t}
:

4:

Use the ERM oracle to compute
h_{t}=\mathrm{ERM}(D,\lambda_{t})
.

5:

Auditor runs Hedge to obtain
\lambda_{t+1}
:

6:

\lambda_{t+1}=\mathrm{Hedge}(c_{1:t})
where
c_{t}(\lambda_{s,i,j,a_{i}})=\lambda_{s,i,j,a_{i}}s{\mathchoice{\left(\mathbb{E}_{h}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h_{t}(x)-y)\cdot\tilde{b}(h(x),a)]-\gamma\right)}{(\mathbb{E}_{h}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h_{t}(x)-y)\cdot\tilde{b}(h(x),a)]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{h}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h_{t}(x)-y)\cdot\tilde{b}(h(x),a)]-\gamma)}{(\mathbb{E}_{h}\mathbb{E}_{D}[(h_{t}(x)-y)\cdot\tilde{b}(h(x),a)]-\gamma)}}
.

7:

end

for

7:

Output
\hat{f}=\mathrm{Uniform}(h_{1},\cdots,h_{T})
.

F.2
Missing Proofs

We need the following technical lemmas before proving
Theorem

5.1
.

Lemma F.1

(Lipschitzness of single smoothed best response)
.

For any
i\in[N],a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
, we have that the function
\tilde{b}_{i}(\cdot,a_{i})
is
2\eta L
-Lipschitz in the
L_{\infty}
norm.

Proof.

For any
z\in[-1,1]^{d}
, for simplicity, we drop the subscript
i
. Let
g_{a}(z)\triangleq\exp(\eta v(z,a)),G(z)\triangleq\sum_{a^{\prime}}\exp(\eta v(z,a^{\prime}))
. We have

\displaystyle\nabla_{z}\tilde{b}(z,a)

\displaystyle=\frac{\nabla_{z}g_{a}(z)}{G(z)}-\frac{g_{a}(z)\nabla_{z}G(z)}{G(z)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta g_{a}(z)}{G(z)}\nabla_{z}v(z,a)-\frac{\eta g_{a}(z)}{G(z)}\sum_{a^{\prime}}\frac{g_{a^{\prime}}(z)}{G(z)}\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\eta b(z,a)\nabla_{z}v(z,a)-\sum_{a^{\prime}}b(z,a^{\prime})\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime}).

Therefore for any
z\in[-1,1]^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\left\|\nabla_{z}\tilde{b}(z,a)\right\|}{\|\nabla_{z}\tilde{b}(z,a)\|}{\|\nabla_{z}\tilde{b}(z,a)\|}{\|\nabla_{z}\tilde{b}(z,a)\|}}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\tilde{b}(z,a){\mathchoice{\left({\mathchoice{\left\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\right\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\|}}_{1}+\sum_{a^{\prime}}\tilde{b}(z,a^{\prime}){\mathchoice{\left\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\right\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\|}}_{1}\right)}{({\mathchoice{\left\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\right\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\|}}_{1}+\sum_{a^{\prime}}\tilde{b}(z,a^{\prime}){\mathchoice{\left\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\right\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\|}}_{1})}{({\mathchoice{\left\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\right\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\|}}_{1}+\sum_{a^{\prime}}\tilde{b}(z,a^{\prime}){\mathchoice{\left\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\right\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\|}}_{1})}{({\mathchoice{\left\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\right\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a)\|}}_{1}+\sum_{a^{\prime}}\tilde{b}(z,a^{\prime}){\mathchoice{\left\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\right\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\|}{\|\nabla_{z}v(z,a^{\prime})\|}}_{1})}}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\tilde{b}(z,a)(L+L)\leq 2\eta L.

By mean-value theorem we have that

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{b}(z,a)-\tilde{b}(z^{\prime},a)\right|}{|\tilde{b}(z,a)-\tilde{b}(z^{\prime},a)|}{|\tilde{b}(z,a)-\tilde{b}(z^{\prime},a)|}{|\tilde{b}(z,a)-\tilde{b}(z^{\prime},a)|}}

\displaystyle={\mathchoice{\left|\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{z}\tilde{b}(z^{\prime}+t(z-z^{\prime}),a)\cdot(z-z^{\prime})dt\right|}{|\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{z}\tilde{b}(z^{\prime}+t(z-z^{\prime}),a)\cdot(z-z^{\prime})dt|}{|\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{z}\tilde{b}(z^{\prime}+t(z-z^{\prime}),a)\cdot(z-z^{\prime})dt|}{|\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{z}\tilde{b}(z^{\prime}+t(z-z^{\prime}),a)\cdot(z-z^{\prime})dt|}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,1]}{\mathchoice{\left\|\nabla_{z}\tilde{b}(z^{\prime}+t(z-z^{\prime}),a)\right\|}{\|\nabla_{z}\tilde{b}(z^{\prime}+t(z-z^{\prime}),a)\|}{\|\nabla_{z}\tilde{b}(z^{\prime}+t(z-z^{\prime}),a)\|}{\|\nabla_{z}\tilde{b}(z^{\prime}+t(z-z^{\prime}),a)\|}}_{1}{\mathchoice{\left\|z-z^{\prime}\right\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta L{\mathchoice{\left\|z-z^{\prime}\right\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}}_{\infty}.

∎

Lemma F.2

(Lipschitzness of joint smoothed best response)
.

For any
\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}
, the joint smoothed best response
\tilde{b}(\cdot,\bm{a})
satisfies that

\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}{\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{b}(z,\bm{a})-\tilde{b}(z^{\prime},\bm{a})\right|}{|\tilde{b}(z,\bm{a})-\tilde{b}(z^{\prime},\bm{a})|}{|\tilde{b}(z,\bm{a})-\tilde{b}(z^{\prime},\bm{a})|}{|\tilde{b}(z,\bm{a})-\tilde{b}(z^{\prime},\bm{a})|}}\leq 2\eta mNL{\mathchoice{\left\|z-z^{\prime}\right\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}}_{\infty}.

Proof.

For any predictions
z,z^{\prime}\in[-1,1]^{d}
and joint action
\bm{a}=(a_{1},\cdots,a_{N})
, denote
b_{i}(z,a_{i})
as
u_{i}(a_{i})
and
b_{i}(z^{\prime},a_{i})
as
v_{i}(a_{i})

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}{\mathchoice{\left|b(z,\bm{a})-b(z^{\prime},\bm{a})\right|}{|b(z,\bm{a})-b(z^{\prime},\bm{a})|}{|b(z,\bm{a})-b(z^{\prime},\bm{a})|}{|b(z,\bm{a})-b(z^{\prime},\bm{a})|}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}{\mathchoice{\left|\prod_{i\in[N]}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i\in[N]}v_{i}(a_{i})\right|}{|\prod_{i\in[N]}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i\in[N]}v_{i}(a_{i})|}{|\prod_{i\in[N]}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i\in[N]}v_{i}(a_{i})|}{|\prod_{i\in[N]}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i\in[N]}v_{i}(a_{i})|}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}{\mathchoice{\left|\prod_{i\in[N]}u_{i}(a_{i})-v_{1}(a_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})+v_{1}(a_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i\in[N]}v_{i}(a_{i})\right|}{|\prod_{i\in[N]}u_{i}(a_{i})-v_{1}(a_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})+v_{1}(a_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i\in[N]}v_{i}(a_{i})|}{|\prod_{i\in[N]}u_{i}(a_{i})-v_{1}(a_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})+v_{1}(a_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i\in[N]}v_{i}(a_{i})|}{|\prod_{i\in[N]}u_{i}(a_{i})-v_{1}(a_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})+v_{1}(a_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i\in[N]}v_{i}(a_{i})|}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}{\mathchoice{\left|(u_{1}(a_{1})-v_{1}(a_{1}))\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})+v_{1}(a_{1}){\mathchoice{\left(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i})\right)}{(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}{(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}{(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}}\right|}{|(u_{1}(a_{1})-v_{1}(a_{1}))\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})+v_{1}(a_{1}){\mathchoice{\left(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i})\right)}{(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}{(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}{(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}}|}{|(u_{1}(a_{1})-v_{1}(a_{1}))\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})+v_{1}(a_{1}){\mathchoice{\left(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i})\right)}{(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}{(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}{(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}}|}{|(u_{1}(a_{1})-v_{1}(a_{1}))\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})+v_{1}(a_{1}){\mathchoice{\left(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i})\right)}{(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}{(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}{(\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=2}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}}|}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}\Bigg\lvert(u_{1}(a_{1})-v_{1}(a_{1}))\prod_{i=2}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})+v_{1}(a_{1})(u_{2}(a_{2})-v_{2}(a_{2}))\prod_{i=3}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})

\displaystyle+v_{1}(a_{1})v_{2}(a_{2}){\mathchoice{\left(\prod_{i=3}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=3}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i})\right)}{(\prod_{i=3}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=3}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}{(\prod_{i=3}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=3}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}{(\prod_{i=3}^{N}u_{i}(a_{i})-\prod_{i=3}^{N}v_{i}(a_{i}))}}\Bigg\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\vdots

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}{\mathchoice{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}v_{j}(a_{j})(u_{i}(a_{i})-v_{i}(a_{i}))\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}u_{j}(a_{j})\right|}{|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}v_{j}(a_{j})(u_{i}(a_{i})-v_{i}(a_{i}))\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}u_{j}(a_{j})|}{|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}v_{j}(a_{j})(u_{i}(a_{i})-v_{i}(a_{i}))\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}u_{j}(a_{j})|}{|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}v_{j}(a_{j})(u_{i}(a_{i})-v_{i}(a_{i}))\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}u_{j}(a_{j})|}}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}2\eta L{\mathchoice{\left\|z-z^{\prime}\right\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}}_{\infty}\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}{\mathchoice{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}v_{j}(a_{j})\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}u_{j}(a_{j})\right|}{|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}v_{j}(a_{j})\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}u_{j}(a_{j})|}{|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}v_{j}(a_{j})\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}u_{j}(a_{j})|}{|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}v_{j}(a_{j})\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}u_{j}(a_{j})|}}

\displaystyle=2\eta L{\mathchoice{\left\|z-z^{\prime}\right\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}}_{\infty}\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}v_{j}(a_{j})\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}u_{j}(a_{j})

\displaystyle=2\eta L{\mathchoice{\left\|z-z^{\prime}\right\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}}_{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}v_{j}(a_{j})\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}u_{j}(a_{j})

\displaystyle=2\eta L{\mathchoice{\left\|z-z^{\prime}\right\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}}_{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}\sum_{\bm{a}_{-i}}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}v_{j}(a_{j})\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}u_{j}(a_{j})

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}2\eta L{\mathchoice{\left\|z-z^{\prime}\right\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}}_{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}1

\displaystyle=2\eta mNL{\mathchoice{\left\|z-z^{\prime}\right\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}}_{\infty}

where
(a)
holds because of
Lemma

F.1
and
(b)
holds because
\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}v_{j}(a_{j})\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}u_{j}(a_{j})
can be viewed as the probability density function of
\bm{a}_{-i}
and then
\sum_{\bm{a}_{-i}}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}v_{j}(a_{j})\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}u_{j}(a_{j})=1
.
∎

Lemma F.3
.

Fix a class of deterministic predictors
\mathcal{H}
. For any distribution
\mathcal{D}
, let
D\sim\mathcal{D}^{n}
be a dataset consisting of
n
samples
(x^{(i)},y^{(i)})
sampled i.i.d. from
\mathcal{D}
. Then for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, with
1-\delta
, for every

, we have

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}_{h\sim f}{\mathchoice{\left[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot\tilde{b}(h(x),\bm{a})\right]}{[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot\tilde{b}(h(x),\bm{a})]}{[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot\tilde{b}(h(x),\bm{a})]}{[\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}}u(\bm{a},y)\cdot\tilde{b}(h(x),\bm{a})]}}-\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{D}\mathbb{E}_{h\sim
